### Task 6: builders as transforms

**Files:**
- Create: `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py`
- Test: `tests/test_render.py` (new — `render.py`'s own test file, 22 tests by the end of Commit A; `tests/test_objects.py` is Task 1's and is not touched)
- Modify: `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py` (every single-workload builder rewritten to draw from `e.objects` via `render.py`/`rules.py`; `_SharedOrigin`, `_render_shared_origin`, the four `shared_origin*` wrappers, `_multi_objects`, `multi`; deletes `_swapped_candidates`, `_decoy_cause` (Step 31) and `_candidates`, `_reads` (Step 36, once `multi` -- their last caller -- is rewritten))
- Modify: `tests/test_cases.py` (one new test per rewritten builder, plus the generator/meta-era additions)
- Modify: `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/generate.py` (`probe_sets()`: `entry.losers`→`entry.objects`, `with_losers`→`with_objects`; a new training-only `separate`-label `multi()` call inserted after the existing multi loop)
- Modify: `tests/test_generator_structure.py` (a structural test on `probe_sets()`'s source; `shared_origin_decoy_probe` added to `EVAL_ONLY`)
- Modify: `tests/test_generate.py` (two pin-only tests proving `generate()` writes nothing on a builder's first error; the job-1/job-2/job-3 population-count pin; the fact-1 down-node pin; the two registry-events-read consistency pins)
- Modify: `tests/test_propagation.py` (tests for `_render_shared_origin`'s object-derived meta and the four wrappers' merged meta)
- Modify: `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/entries_slugs.py` (Task 5 carry-over: `networkpolicy-deny-all` and `coredns-corefile-broken` `losers` tuples corrected)
- Modify: `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/propagation.py:335-350,510-525` (Task 5 carry-over: `node-not-ready` victim 1 rewritten Pending→RunContainerError; `registry-unreachable` victim 3 narrowed to plain `ImagePullBackOff`)
- Modify: `src/kubeagent_verdict/contract.py` (`SYSTEM_PROMPT` loaded from `contract/system_prompt.txt` instead of an inline triple-quoted string)
- Move+modify: `contract/system_prompt.txt` (`git mv -f`'d in from `contract/capture/out/system_prompt.txt`, replacing the stale v1.23.0 text with Task 2's v1.24.0 capture)
- Delete: `tests/test_capture_staging.py` (the staging directory `contract/capture/out/` it tested goes empty and is removed)
- Modify: `tests/test_shared_origin_training.py:722,734-736` (`FROZEN_253_SHA256`/`EVAL_SET_SHA256` re-measured and re-pinned; the training-only `separate`-prompt pin test added)
- Modify: `tests/test_evidence_overlap.py` (the six `DECLARED` evidence-reuse pairs re-measured and re-pinned)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - Task 1 (`dataset/objects.py`): `Object`, `Fresh`, `refute()`, `drop()`, `unverify()` — re-exported by `render.py` and drawn from directly by `draw_ending`.
  - Task 3 (`dataset/rules.py`): `rules.attribute()`, `rules.decide()`, `rules.label()`, `rules.Result` (`.decided`, `.cause`, `.outcome`, `.evidence`), `rules.Candidate`, `rules.Decision`.
  - Task 4 (`contract.py`): `contract.Workload`, `contract.EvidenceRead`, `contract.build_user_message()` (imported as `c` inside `render.py`: `c.Workload`, `c.EvidenceRead`).
  - Task 5 (`dataset/entries_slugs.py` / `propagation.py`): `CatalogEntry.objects` — the declared-object tuple every rewritten builder now reads instead of `CatalogEntry.losers`, which stays on the dataclass as documentation only.
- Produces (used by Tasks 7-10):
  - `render.py`'s twelve exported names: `apply_budget`, `bind`, `check_prompt_size`, `draw_ending`, `drop`, `object_reads`, `prompt_meta`, `refute`, `registry_events_read`, `render_workload`, `unverify`, `workload_meta`.
  - `render.MAX_READS = 8` and `render.MAX_PROMPT_BYTES` — the gather-budget and prompt-size caps every builder now enforces through `apply_budget`/`check_prompt_size`.
  - `workload_meta()`'s seven-key dict shape: `job`, `decided`, `decided_cause`, `decided_outcome`, `decided_evidence`, `expected_cause`, `own_cause_keywords`.
  - `prompt_meta()`'s dict shape: every `workload_meta()` entry plus `label` and `decoy_by_workload`.
  - Per-example `meta["workloads"]` (one `workload_meta()` dict per workload) and `meta["label"]` (`"shared"`/`"separate"`/`"none"`, from `rules.label()`), populated on EVERY row without exception: every `shared_origin*` row, every `multi()` row, and every single-workload row too, where `workloads` is a one-entry dict keyed `"{ns}/{name}"` and `label` is always `"none"`. Job 3 is never derived from whether these keys are present -- it is derived from `len(meta["workloads"]) >= 2` -- because they are present on all 263 rows.
  - Re-pinned `FROZEN_253_SHA256`, `EVAL_SET_SHA256` (`tests/test_shared_origin_training.py`) and the six re-measured `DECLARED` evidence-reuse pairs (`tests/test_evidence_overlap.py`) — the actual pasted values are not fixed by this plan; they are whatever Steps 46-47 measure against the rewritten generator.
  - Pinned population counts (`tests/test_generate.py`): measured `job1`/`job2` literals, `job3_prompts == 39`, `job3_labels == {"shared": 5, "separate": 0, "none": 34}`.
  - `contract.SYSTEM_PROMPT`, now loaded from `contract/system_prompt.txt`, whose bytes this task moves over the stale v1.23.0 text — the value gains the "decided by rules" paragraph.


NOTE: the new test file this part creates is `tests/test_render.py`, named for
the module it covers the way `tests/test_cases.py` covers `cases.py`. Task 1's
`tests/test_objects.py` is untouched here; this task adds nothing to it.

NOTE: `apply_budget` takes `(objects, *, workload_order, allow_overflow,
entry_or_scenario_key)`. The `truncated` builder passes an explicit
`allow_overflow=True` flag to `apply_budget`, and the read-count
`ValueError` names an `entry_or_scenario_key` — both are required keyword
parameters. `workload_order` is typed `tuple[int, ...]`, one 0-based
workload index per object in `objects` (a single-workload caller passes
`(0,) * len(objects)`), so the walk can do a stable sort by `(phase,
workload_order[i])` — this is the concrete reading of "events first, per
workload, in workload declaration order... then node describes (all
workloads' node objects, in the same left-to-right order)... then PVC
describes."

NOTE: `object_reads` takes `(objects, *, ns, pod, image="")`. It must call
`registry_events_read`, which needs `image`, so `image` is a keyword
parameter with an empty-string default.

NOTE: `render_workload` returns a 3-tuple — `(Workload, tuple[EvidenceRead,
...], Result)`, not the earlier-drafted `tuple[c.Workload, rules.Result]`.
`contract.render_candidates` is the STRING renderer `build_user_message`
calls later (it takes a whole `tuple[Workload, ...]` and returns `str`; it
does not build a Workload's `.candidates` field), and `contract.Workload`
has no `reads` field to carry a read tuple in — so `render_workload` builds
`.candidates` itself from `rules.attribute()` + `rules.decide()` (zipping
each `rules.Candidate` with its `rules.Decision` when one exists) and
returns the read tuple it built as its own second element, so the reads
are actually reachable by the caller. `render_workload` does not itself
call `bind`/`draw_ending`/`apply_budget` — it is the single entry point
every builder calls once it has finished transforming its object tuple,
and those three all run in the builder, before `objects` is handed in.
`rng` is accepted (matching the other helpers' shape) but unused — this is
documented in the docstring rather than silently dropped, since every draw
already happened upstream. `findings`, `confidence`, `network_policies`,
`rollout`, `ready`, `desired` stay outside this task's scope (unchanged by
Task 6), so this part's `Workload` leaves them at neutral defaults (`()`,
`""`, `None`, `0`) — a caller that needs the full prompt-ready Workload
merges `.candidates`/`.decided*` from this return into its own
`_workload(...)` call rather than using this Workload verbatim.

NOTE: `workload_meta`'s `decided_cause` key is `result.cause when decided,
else ""` — `rules.Result` has no `decided_cause` field, only `.cause`, and
the meta value is an empty string (never `None`) when the workload is not
decided. `decided_outcome` and `decided_evidence` follow the same rule
against `result.outcome`/`result.evidence`, all falling back to `""`.
`own_cause_keywords` is a required keyword parameter, `list[str]`, passed
through unchanged — `workload_meta` does not compute it and does not
default it, so every caller decides its own value (per the interface
sheet: non-empty only when `job == 2` and `expected_cause` is a named
cause, `[]` on a decided workload and on `none_of_these`).

NOTE: `prompt_meta` takes `workload_metas: dict[str, dict]`, keyed by
`"{ns}/{name}"` — matching what every builder actually assembles and what
every downstream reader (`.values()`, `.keys()`, `.items()`) expects.
`prompt_meta` does not itself call `workload_meta` and only adds `label`/
`decoy_by_workload` — both already fully computed by the caller. It uses
`workload_metas` for one defensible check — refusing an empty dict, since a
prompt with zero workloads is not a shape any builder produces — rather
than recomputing `label`, which this function does not do.

NOTE: the byte-size `ValueError` names `entry_or_scenario_key` exactly
once, matching `apply_budget`'s own single-naming style (`f"entry
{entry_or_scenario_key}: ..."`, never a second, redundant `!r` repeat of
the same value) — raised by the helper `check_prompt_size`, a function a
builder calls after `build_user_message`, not code inside
`render_workload`, since the check is raised by `render_workload`'s
caller.

NOTE: `draw_ending` draws from the interface sheet's own Option-A list
(notes-spec.md, verbatim): node ∈ {refuted, lease, read_failed}; pvc ∈
{refuted, read_failed}; registry ∈ {refuted, auth, no_event}. A decoy's
draw is never "confirmed" (left as declared) — the interface sheet names
exactly these endings per kind, with no as-declared option, because a
decoy that stays as declared could independently satisfy
`rules.decide()`'s first-CONFIRMED-wins rule and win outright, which no
decoy may do. Registry's `read_failed` is dropped from the registry set
specifically, since the interface sheet states "registry `read_failed` are
never drawn."

```bash
cat > tests/test_render.py <<'PYEOF'
"""Unit tests for render.py's helpers — no builder or catalog calls.

Every fixture Object here is built by hand; none of these tests import
cases.py or generate.py. That is deliberate: this file is what proves
Commit A (render.py alone) is additive-only — nothing here can move either
frozen hash.
"""

import random

from kubeagent_verdict import contract as c
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import objects as o
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import render
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import rules


def test_bind_formats_every_string_field():
    obj = o.Object(kind="node", name="{node}", scan_reason="NotReady",
                    placement="on", fresh=o.Fresh(ready="False"), intent="cause")
    names = {"ns": "shop", "name": "api", "pod": "api-0", "container": "api",
              "init_container": "init", "node": "worker-1", "pvc": "data-0",
              "image": "registry.example.com/api:1", "restarts": 5}
    bound = render.bind(obj, names)
    assert bound.name == "worker-1"
    assert bound.kind == "node"
    assert bound.scan_reason == "NotReady"
    assert bound.placement == "on"
    assert bound.intent == "cause"
    assert bound.fresh == obj.fresh
    assert obj.name == "{node}"


def test_bind_leaves_a_literal_name_unchanged():
    obj = o.Object(kind="pvc", name="aux-0", scan_reason="ProvisioningFailed",
                    placement="unmounted", fresh=o.Fresh(how="read", phase="Pending"))
    names = {"ns": "shop", "name": "api", "pod": "api-0", "container": "api",
              "init_container": "init", "node": "worker-1", "pvc": "data-0",
              "image": "registry.example.com/api:1", "restarts": 0}
    bound = render.bind(obj, names)
```

- [ ] **Step 2: `bind()` — implement**

Create `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py`:

```bash
cat > src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py <<'PYEOF'
"""Render kubeagent-shaped candidates and reads from declared objects.

A case builder in cases.py declares one CatalogEntry/scenario's decoy
objects, transforms each with an Option-A ending (as declared, refuted, or
unverified), then hands the finished tuple to this module to turn into the
EvidenceRead/Candidate shapes contract.py's prompt assembly expects. No
builder ever imports objects.py directly — cases.py imports everything it
needs from here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import dataclasses

from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import names
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import objects as o
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset.objects import drop

# render.py's public surface. Each step that adds a new function or a new
# re-exported name appends it here, so ruff's F401 (unused import) never
# has a window where an already-imported name looks unused.
__all__ = ["bind", "drop"]

MAX_READS = 8
MAX_PROMPT_BYTES = 64 * 1024

# The closed set of Option-A endings draw_ending() may pick, per kind. Each
# name past "refuted" is the `how` unverify() takes for that kind — matches
# objects.py's own unverify() vocabulary (node: read_failed, lease; pvc:
# read_failed; registry: read_failed, auth, no_event), minus registry's
# read_failed: the interface sheet says registry read_failed is never
# drawn. There is no "confirmed"/as-declared entry: a decoy left as
# declared could independently satisfy rules.decide()'s first-CONFIRMED-
# wins rule and win outright, which no decoy may do.
ENDINGS = {
    "node": ("refuted", "lease", "read_failed"),
    "pvc": ("refuted", "read_failed"),
    "registry": ("refuted", "auth", "no_event"),
}

# Nine always-ruled-out PVC decoys `truncated` appends to overflow the 8-read
# budget. Never drawn by any rng (fresh.how="not_read" — draw_ending is never
# called on them), so no seed's draw sequence moves when they are added.
PAD_PVC_OBJECTS = tuple(
    o.Object(kind="pvc", name=pad_name, scan_reason="ProvisioningFailed",
             placement="unmounted", fresh=o.Fresh(how="not_read"), intent="decoy")
    for pad_name in names.PAD_PVCS
)


def bind(obj: o.Object, names: dict) -> o.Object:
    """Fill in an object's `{ns}`/`{pod}`/... placeholders from `names`.

    `names` is the same dict shape `names.draw(rng)` returns. Every string
    field on `obj` is run through `str.format(**names)`; every other field
    (bool, tuple, the nested `Fresh`) passes through unchanged. `obj` is
    never mutated — a new Object is returned. A name with no placeholders
    (a literal PVC name such as one of `names.PAD_PVCS`) passes through
    `.format()` as a no-op.
    """
    changes = {}
    for field in dataclasses.fields(obj):
        value = getattr(obj, field.name)
        if isinstance(value, str):
            changes[field.name] = value.format(**names)
    return dataclasses.replace(obj, **changes)
PYEOF
```

Run: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_render.py`
Expected: `2 passed`

Run: `ruff check src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py tests/test_render.py`
Expected: `All checks passed!`

`drop` earns its place in `__all__` the moment it is imported: nothing in
`render.py`'s own body ever calls it directly — its only job here is to be
re-exported so `cases.py` can import it without reaching into `objects.py`
itself — so ruff's F401 (unused import) would otherwise flag it at this
very check. `random`, `contract`, and `rules` are left out of this file's
import block entirely for now; each is added in the step that first uses
it (Steps 4, 8, and 10), so no import ever sits unused between when it is
written and when it is used.

gather.go's real order is per workload, not one shared "describes" pass
across workloads. For each workload, kubeagent does three reads in
strict order — events, then describes, then log causes — before moving
to the next workload (gather.go:71-155, the outer `for _, w := range
scoped` loop). Within the describe step, node and PVC reads are not
split into two phases: the loop walks `for _, h := range
w.RootCauseTrace` (gather.go:92) in the trace's own order, so node and
PVC candidates interleave exactly as the trace lists them for that one
workload. A registry candidate gets no describe read at all — gather.go
skips it with `continue` when `h.Kind != "node" && h.Kind != "pvc"`
(gather.go:99-100, comment `// registry: no object to read`). Its only
evidence is the workload's events read, which is unconditional and
always happens first, before any describe (gather.go:75-90). Describe
reads dedupe globally across workloads, keyed `h.Kind + "/" + ns + "/" +
h.Object` (gather.go:106), so the same node or PVC named by two
workloads' traces is described once, not twice. The 8-read budget is one
counter (`spent`, gather.go:61) shared by the whole gather; the outer
loop checks it before each workload's events read (gather.go:72-74), so
once it is spent, every remaining workload gets no read at all, events
included.

`apply_budget` in this task never has to compare one workload's node
read against another workload's PVC read: every call site hands it a
single workload's own object tuple (`_decoy_result` and `truncated`,
both keyed to one `n.ns/n.name`). So the cross-workload half of this
question never comes up here. What does carry over is the in-workload
fact above: describes should follow declared order, not a hard
node-then-PVC split. `apply_budget`'s `_PHASE` sort (registry=0, node=1,
pvc=2) is a simplification of that — it puts the registry/events slot
first, which matches gather.go's pass one, and it never disagrees with
the true order for any scenario this task builds, because no entry here
declares both a node candidate and a PVC candidate close enough to the
8-read line for kind order to change which one gets cut.

`render_workload` is implemented and unit-tested here as "the single
entry point every builder calls once it has finished transforming its
object tuple". The case-builder rewrites later in this task call it
directly (see the `_render_shared_origin` and `multi` rewrites below)
rather than assembling `contract.Workload`/`contract.Candidate` by hand.

```python
import dataclasses
import random

from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import names
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import objects as o
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset.objects import drop, refute, unverify

# render.py's public surface. Each step that adds a new function or a new
# re-exported name appends it here, so ruff's F401 (unused import) never
```

Add to `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py` (after `bind`):

```python
def draw_ending(obj: o.Object, rng: random.Random) -> o.Object:
    """Draw one Option-A ending for `obj` and return the transformed copy.

    Picks uniformly from ENDINGS[obj.kind] with rng.choice(). "refuted"
    calls refute(obj). Every other name is a `how` string passed straight
    to unverify(obj, how). There is no as-declared choice: every decoy
    this function draws for comes back either refuted or unverified, never
    left as declared.
    """
    choice = rng.choice(ENDINGS[obj.kind])
    if choice == "refuted":
        return refute(obj)
    return unverify(obj, choice)
```

- [ ] **Step 5: `apply_budget()` — failing test**

`apply_budget` walks a workload's declared objects in kubeagent's own
gather order (registry events first, then node describes, then PVC
describes, tie-broken by `workload_order`), then applies the 8-read
budget: any object past the 8th in that order gets its `fresh` replaced
with `Fresh(how="not_read")` — the same outcome `rules.py`'s node/pvc/
registry checkers already treat as `"not re-read: the read budget was
spent first"`. An `intent="cause"` object that would be silently starved
this way is a scenario-authoring bug, so that case raises instead of
starving quietly — unless the caller passes `allow_overflow=True`, which
is exactly what a scenario built to TEACH "the model never got the
cause's fresh read" needs.

Append to `tests/test_render.py`:

```bash
cat >> tests/test_render.py <<'PYEOF'


def _node_decoy(n):
    return o.Object(kind="node", name=f"worker-{n}", scan_reason="NotReady",
                     placement="on", fresh=o.Fresh(how="read", ready="True"))


def test_apply_budget_marks_a_decoy_past_the_budget_not_read():

        o.Object(kind="pvc", name="aux-0", scan_reason="ProvisioningFailed",
                  placement="unmounted", fresh=o.Fresh(how="read", phase="Pending")),
    )
    out = render.apply_budget(objects, workload_order=(0,) * 9,
                               entry_or_scenario_key="e1")

    assert out[8].kind == "pvc"
    assert out[8].fresh.how == "not_read"
    assert out[8].fresh.phase == ""
    assert out[0].fresh.how == "read"


def test_apply_budget_raises_when_a_cause_would_be_starved():
    cause = o.Object(kind="pvc", name="data-0", scan_reason="FailedBinding",
                      placement="mounted", fresh=o.Fresh(how="read", phase="Pending"),
                      intent="cause")
```

- [ ] **Step 6: `apply_budget()` — implement**

Also widen `__all__` (last touched in Step 4) to add this step's new name:

Existing line in `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py`:

```python
__all__ = ["bind", "draw_ending", "drop", "refute", "unverify"]
```

Replace with:

```python
__all__ = ["apply_budget", "bind", "draw_ending", "drop", "refute", "unverify"]
```

Add to `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py` (after `draw_ending`):

```python
_PHASE = {"registry": 0, "node": 1, "pvc": 2}


def apply_budget(
    objects: tuple[o.Object, ...],
    *,
    workload_order: tuple[int, ...],
    entry_or_scenario_key: str,
    allow_overflow: bool = False,
) -> tuple[o.Object, ...]:
    """Reorder into gather order, then apply the 8-read budget.

    Walks `objects` in kubeagent's own gather order — registry events
    first, then node describes, then PVC describes, ties broken by
    `workload_order[i]` — and returns them in that order. Any object past
    the 8th (MAX_READS) has its `fresh` replaced with `Fresh(how="not_read")`,
    matching what a real gather does when the read budget runs out before
    it reaches an object. An `intent="cause"` object that would be starved
    this way raises ValueError, since a scenario that silently loses its
    own cause's evidence is almost always an authoring mistake — pass
    `allow_overflow=True` for the one builder that starves the cause on
    purpose.
    """
    order = sorted(range(len(objects)),
                    key=lambda i: (_PHASE[objects[i].kind], workload_order[i]))
    ordered = tuple(objects[i] for i in order)
    out = []
    for idx, obj in enumerate(ordered):
        if idx < MAX_READS:
            out.append(obj)
            continue
        if obj.intent == "cause" and not allow_overflow:
            raise ValueError(
                f"entry {entry_or_scenario_key}: the {MAX_READS}-read budget "
                f"would leave {obj.kind}/{obj.name} (intent=cause) unread; "
                "pass allow_overflow=True if that is the point"
            )
        out.append(dataclasses.replace(obj, fresh=o.Fresh(how="not_read")))
    return tuple(out)
```

project generates has reason `"Failed"`, message `Failed to pull image

Add to `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py` (after `apply_budget`):

```python
def registry_events_read(
    obj: o.Object,
    *,
    ns: str,
    pod: str,
    image: str,
    own_line: str | None = None,
) -> c.EvidenceRead:
    """Build the `events {ns}/{pod}` read for a registry object.

    Mirrors kubeagent's own formatEvents: no literal (the "no_event"
    ending) reads as "no events for ns/pod"; any other literal reads as
    one "Failed:" line naming it. `own_line`, when given, appends a SECOND
    "Failed:" line built from it, after the object's own line — this is
    the contradiction_probe shape, where the events read carries both the
    unverified-auth literal (first, so classifyPullEvents settles on
    "auth") and the entry's own image-error literal (second, the cause the
    read still points at).
    """
    label = f"events {ns}/{pod}"
    if obj.fresh.literal == "" and own_line is None:
        return c.EvidenceRead(label=label, content=f"no events for {ns}/{pod}")
    content = (
        f"events for {ns}/{pod}:\n"
        f'  Failed: Failed to pull image "{image}": {obj.fresh.literal} (x4)\n'
    )
    if own_line is not None:
        content += f'  Failed: Failed to pull image "{image}": {own_line} (x4)\n'
    return c.EvidenceRead(label=label, content=content)
```

```python
from kubeagent_verdict import contract as c
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import names, rules
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import objects as o
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset.objects import drop, refute, unverify

# render.py's public surface. Each step that adds a new function or a new
# re-exported name appends it here, so ruff's F401 (unused import) never
# has a window where an already-imported name looks unused.
```

Add to `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py` (after `registry_events_read`):

```python
def object_reads(
    objects: tuple[o.Object, ...],
    *,
    ns: str,
    pod: str,
    image: str = "",
) -> tuple[c.EvidenceRead, ...]:
    """Turn ended objects into the EvidenceRead tuple a prompt renders.

    Node and PVC objects reuse rules.read_text unchanged. Registry objects
    go through registry_events_read (image is only used for those). An
    object with fresh.how == "not_read" produces no read — the read
    budget never reached it, so there is nothing to show.
    """
    reads = []
    for obj in objects:
        if obj.fresh.how == "not_read":
            continue
        if obj.kind == "registry":
            reads.append(registry_events_read(obj, ns=ns, pod=pod, image=image))
            continue
        label, content = rules.read_text(obj, ns=ns, pod=pod)
        reads.append(c.EvidenceRead(label=label, content=content))
    return tuple(reads)
```

Add to `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py` (after `object_reads`):

```python
def render_workload(
    objects: tuple[o.Object, ...],
    *,
    ns: str,
    name: str,
    pod: str,
    image: str,
    issue: str,
    kind: str,
    status: str,
    rng: random.Random | None = None,
) -> tuple[c.Workload, tuple[c.EvidenceRead, ...], rules.Result]:
    """Assemble a partial Workload, its reads, and the rules.Result.

    `objects` must already carry each object's final drawn ending — this
    function does not call bind/draw_ending/apply_budget; the builder runs
    those before handing objects in here. `rng` is accepted for signature
    symmetry with this module's other helpers but is unused: every draw
    already happened upstream.

    The returned Workload fills only the fields this module owns —
    namespace, name, kind, status, candidates, decided, decided_cause,
    decided_outcome. findings, confidence, network_policies, rollout,
    ready, and desired stay at their dataclass defaults; a caller building
    a full prompt-ready Workload merges those in separately.
    """
    candidates = rules.attribute(objects, ns=ns, pod=pod, issue=issue)
    result = rules.decide(candidates)
    decisions = {d.candidate: d for d in result.decisions}
    contract_candidates = tuple(
        c.Candidate(
            cause=cand.cause,
            verdict=cand.verdict,
            reason=cand.reason,
            fresh_read_outcome=(
                decisions[cand.cause].outcome if cand.cause in decisions else ""
            ),
            fresh_read_evidence=(
                decisions[cand.cause].evidence if cand.cause in decisions else ""
            ),
        )
        for cand in candidates
    )
    reads = object_reads(objects, ns=ns, pod=pod, image=image)
    workload = c.Workload(
        namespace=ns, name=name, kind=kind, ready=0, desired=0, status=status,
        restarts=0, findings=(), candidates=contract_candidates,
        decided=result.decided, decided_cause=result.cause,
        decided_outcome=result.outcome,
    )
    return workload, reads, result
```

Add to `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py` (after `render_workload`):

```python
def workload_meta(result: rules.Result, *, expected_cause: str,
                  own_cause_keywords: list[str]) -> dict:
    """Build the seven-key per-workload meta dict: job, decided,
    decided_cause, decided_outcome, decided_evidence, expected_cause,
    own_cause_keywords.
    `job` is derived from `result` itself — 1 when the workload decided, 2
    when it did not — never passed in by the caller. The four decided_*
    keys read straight off `result`, falling back to Result's own ""
    defaults when the workload was never decided. `own_cause_keywords` is
    passed through as given — job 2's grading list for this one
    workload's own cause; the caller is responsible for the
    interface-sheet rule that it is non-empty only when job == 2 and
    expected_cause is a named cause (never on none_of_these, never on a
    decided workload).
    """
    return {
        "job": 1 if result.decided else 2,
        "decided": result.decided,
        "decided_cause": result.cause,
        "decided_outcome": result.outcome,
        "decided_evidence": result.evidence,
        "expected_cause": expected_cause,
        "own_cause_keywords": own_cause_keywords,
    }
```

Add to `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py` (after `workload_meta`):

```python
def prompt_meta(
    workload_metas: dict[str, dict],
    *,
    label: str,
    decoy_by_workload: dict,
) -> dict:
    """Add the two prompt-level meta keys to an already-built dict of
    per-workload meta dicts, keyed by "{ns}/{name}". Does not call
    workload_meta itself — both `label` (from
    rules.label(rules.shared(results))) and `decoy_by_workload` are
    computed by the caller.
    """
    if not workload_metas:
        raise ValueError("prompt_meta: workload_metas must not be empty")
    return {
        "workloads": workload_metas,
        "label": label,
        "decoy_by_workload": decoy_by_workload,
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 17: `check_prompt_size()` — failing test**

`contract.build_user_message` silently truncates an oversized prompt at
render time (the same policy as kubeagent's own runtime `local.go`
`maxPromptBytes` cap). That is wrong for a training example: a silently
truncated prompt would train the model on evidence the entry's author
never saw. `check_prompt_size` is the build-time refusal a builder calls
right after `build_user_message`, before writing the example out.

```bash
    big = "x" * (render.MAX_PROMPT_BYTES + 1)
    try:
        render.check_prompt_size(big, entry_or_scenario_key="e1")
        raise AssertionError("expected ValueError")
    except ValueError as err:
        assert str(err) == (
            f"entry e1: prompt is {len(big)} bytes, over the "
            f"{render.MAX_PROMPT_BYTES}-byte cap"
        )
PYEOF
```

Add to `src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/render.py` (after `prompt_meta`):

```python
def check_prompt_size(prompt: str, *, entry_or_scenario_key: str) -> None:
    """Raise if `prompt`, UTF-8 encoded, is over MAX_PROMPT_BYTES.

    Call this right after contract.build_user_message, before writing an
    example out — build_user_message itself only truncates, it never
    raises (that is kubeagent's own runtime policy; this module's job is
    to refuse the truncation before it ever reaches a training example).
    """
    size = len(prompt.encode("utf-8"))
    if size > MAX_PROMPT_BYTES:
        raise ValueError(
            f"entry {entry_or_scenario_key}: prompt is {size} bytes, over "
            f"the {MAX_PROMPT_BYTES}-byte cap"
        )
```

- [ ] **Step 20: `attributed` — draw endings on the declared decoys, decide from them, keep the winner as prose**

From this step on, builders read their candidate menu from `CatalogEntry.objects`
(Task 5's field) through `render.py`'s helpers (committed at Step 19), instead of
from `_candidates`/`e.losers`. First widen the import block.

Every one of the ten single-workload builders rewritten in this and the following steps
(`attributed`, `injection`, `truncated`, `none_of_these_case`, `own_cause_case`,
`empty_candidates`, `wrong_attribution`, `positional_probe`, `misattribution_probe`,
`contradiction_probe`) ALSO populates `workload_meta`/`prompt_meta` now, not just
`_render_shared_origin` (Step 34) and `multi()`: Task 7's `evaluate()` reads
`meta["workloads"]`/`meta["label"]` on every exam row with no branching on case type
(D4), so a single-workload row must carry them too -- a ONE-ENTRY `workloads` dict keyed
`"{n.ns}/{n.name}"`, and `label="none"` always, since job 3 is derived from
`len(meta["workloads"]) >= 2`, never from a row's case type. Three of the ten
(`attributed`, `injection`, `positional_probe`) get this for free once `_winner_example`
itself is widened below, since all three funnel through it. The other seven build their
`Example` directly and each gains its own small addition in its own step.

```python
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset.generate import Example
```

```python
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset.render import (
    apply_budget,
    bind,
    draw_ending,
    drop,
    object_reads,
    prompt_meta,
    refute,
    registry_events_read,
    unverify,
    workload_meta,

```

Add two shared helpers right after `_confidence` (today's lines 142-143, kept as-is) and
before `_winner_example` (today's line 146). This is new code — `cases.py` has neither
helper today.

```python
def _option_a_menu(n: Names, objects: tuple, rng: random.Random) -> tuple:
    """Bind every declared object to this row's names, then draw one Option-A
    ending per object, in declaration order, from the row's own rng. Used by
    the builders whose decoys are ordinary candidates: `attributed`,
    `injection`, `truncated`, `positional_probe`.
    """
    names = n._asdict()
    return tuple(draw_ending(bind(obj, names), rng) for obj in objects)


def _to_contract_candidates(candidates: tuple, result: rules.Result) -> tuple[c.Candidate, ...]:
    """Convert `rules.Candidate` results into `contract.Candidate`s.

    `rules.Candidate` and `contract.Candidate` are DIFFERENT types: rules'
    version carries `.obj`/`.ns` for `decide()`'s own bookkeeping, while
    contract's version carries `.fresh_read_outcome`/`.fresh_read_evidence`
    for the rendered "fresh read: ..." line. `rules.decide` never re-checks
    a ruled-out candidate, so one with no matching `Decision` keeps both
    fresh-read fields at their `""` default.
    """
    by_cause = {d.candidate: d for d in result.decisions}
    out = []
    for cand in candidates:
        dec = by_cause.get(cand.cause)
        out.append(c.Candidate(cause=cand.cause, verdict=cand.verdict, reason=cand.reason,
                               fresh_read_outcome=dec.outcome if dec else "",
                               fresh_read_evidence=dec.evidence if dec else ""))
    return tuple(out)


def _decoy_result(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, menu: tuple) -> tuple[tuple[c.Candidate, ...], rules.Result]:
    """Apply the read budget to a bound menu of objects, then turn it into
    (candidates, result) via `rules.attribute`/`rules.decide`. The returned
    candidates are already `contract.Candidate`s, converted through
    `_to_contract_candidates`, ready to hand to `_workload`/`_winner_example`.
    Every single-workload builder that reaches the rules pass calls this
    once, after building its own menu.
    """
    budgeted = apply_budget(menu, workload_order=(0,) * len(menu),
                            entry_or_scenario_key=e.key)
    raw = rules.attribute(budgeted, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod, issue=e.issue)
    result = rules.decide(raw)
    return _to_contract_candidates(raw, result), result
```

`_winner_example` is the shared funnel three of the ten single-workload builders still go
through after this task's rewrite: `attributed`, `injection`, `positional_probe`. Widening it
once here (D4) means those three need no edits of their own to gain
`meta["workloads"]`/`meta["label"]` -- every other single-workload builder bypasses this
helper and builds `Example(...)` directly, so each of those gets its own small addition in
its own step below.

Today's `_winner_example` (`src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py`, lines 146-168):

```python
def _winner_example(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, cands: tuple[c.Candidate, ...],
                    reads: tuple[c.EvidenceRead, ...], case: str,
                    extra_meta: dict | None = None) -> Example:
    """Shared shape for every case whose answer is the catalog winner.

    Callers hand in the candidate menu they already rendered rather than
    letting this build one, because _candidates() draws a fresh shuffle on
    every call: building the menu twice would render a prompt from one
    ordering and bank an answer against another.
    """
    conf = _confidence(e)
    w = _workload(e, n, cands, confidence=conf)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    cause = _fmt(e.winner_cause, n)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": cause, "confidence": conf,
             "rationale": _fmt(e.rationale, n)}]
    summary = (f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing: {cause}.\n"
               f"{_fmt(e.recommendation, n).capitalize()}.")
    meta = {"case": case, "entry": e.key,
            "expected_cause": cause, "expected_confidence": conf}
    meta.update(extra_meta or {})
    return Example(case=case, group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta=meta)
```

Replace with:

```python
def _winner_example(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, cands: tuple[c.Candidate, ...],
                    reads: tuple[c.EvidenceRead, ...], case: str,
                    extra_meta: dict | None = None) -> Example:
    """Shared shape for every case whose answer is the catalog winner.

    Callers hand in the candidate menu they already rendered rather than
    letting this build one, because _candidates() draws a fresh shuffle on
    every call: building the menu twice would render a prompt from one
    ordering and bank an answer against another.

    D4: every exam row needs meta["workloads"]/meta["label"], not just
    shared_origin*/multi() rows -- Task 7's evaluate() reads both keys
    unconditionally. A winner-example row is single-workload, so its
    "result" is not the decoy-only rules.Result its caller already computed
    and discarded (the true winner here is always a hand-built Candidate
    literal, never run through rules.decide): it is built fresh here,
    decided=True at this cause, from values this function already has.
    """
    conf = _confidence(e)
    w = _workload(e, n, cands, confidence=conf)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    cause = _fmt(e.winner_cause, n)
    rationale = _fmt(e.rationale, n)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": cause, "confidence": conf,
             "rationale": rationale}]
    summary = (f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing: {cause}.\n"
               f"{_fmt(e.recommendation, n).capitalize()}.")
    key = f"{n.ns}/{n.name}"
    result = rules.Result(decided=True, cause=cause, outcome="confirmed",
                          evidence=rationale, group_key="", group_text="", decisions=())
    wm = workload_meta(result, expected_cause=cause, own_cause_keywords=[])
    decoys = [cand.cause for cand in cands if cand.cause != cause]
    meta = {"case": case, "entry": e.key,
            "expected_cause": cause, "expected_confidence": conf}
    meta.update(prompt_meta({key: wm}, label="none", decoy_by_workload={key: decoys}))
    meta.update(extra_meta or {})
    return Example(case=case, group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta=meta)
```

`meta.update(extra_meta or {})` still runs last, so a caller's `extra_meta` (`injection`'s
`{"injection_payload": payload}`) still wins on any real collision; none of the three keys
`prompt_meta` adds here -- `"workloads"`, `"label"`, `"decoy_by_workload"` -- collides with
`"case"`, `"entry"`, `"expected_cause"`, `"expected_confidence"`, or `"injection_payload"`.
`decoy_by_workload` lists every candidate cause in `cands` other than the winning `cause`
itself -- the same "decoy's cause string, never a raw identifier" meaning `_render_shared_
origin`'s own inline comment establishes for this key elsewhere in this task.

Today's `attributed` (`src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py`, lines 172-173):

```python
def attributed(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    return _winner_example(e, n, _candidates(e, n, rng), _reads(e, n), "attributed")
```

Replace with:

```python
def attributed(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    menu = _option_a_menu(n, e.objects, rng)
    candidates, _result = _decoy_result(e, n, menu)
    winner = c.Candidate(cause=_fmt(e.winner_cause, n), verdict="attributed",
                          reason=_fmt(e.winner_reason, n))
    cands = list(candidates) + [winner]
    rng.shuffle(cands)
    reads = object_reads(menu, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod)
    return _winner_example(e, n, tuple(cands), reads, "attributed")
```

Replace with:

```python
def injection(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, payload: str, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    menu = _option_a_menu(n, e.objects, rng)
    candidates, _result = _decoy_result(e, n, menu)
    winner = c.Candidate(cause=_fmt(e.winner_cause, n), verdict="attributed",
                          reason=_fmt(e.winner_reason, n))
    cands = list(candidates) + [winner]
    rng.shuffle(cands)
    reads = list(object_reads(menu, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod))
    first = reads[0]
    reads[0] = c.EvidenceRead(label=first.label, content=first.content + "\n" + payload)
    return _winner_example(e, n, tuple(cands), tuple(reads), "injection",
                            {"injection_payload": payload})
```

NOTE: every catalog entry `injection` is generated against declares at least one object,

- [ ] **Step 22: `truncated` — 9 `PAD_PVCS` decoys push the real menu past the read budget**

`truncated` needs nine extra, always-ruled-out PVC objects to overflow the 8-read budget.
Builders never import `objects` directly, so this step widens the `render`
import once more to pull in a pre-built constant instead of constructing `Object`s by hand.

Today's import block, after Step 20 (`src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py`, the block
Step 20 just wrote):

```python
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset.render import (
    apply_budget,
    bind,
    draw_ending,
    drop,
    object_reads,
    refute,
    registry_events_read,
    unverify,
)
```

Replace with:

```python
from kubeagent_verdict.dataset.render import (
    PAD_PVC_OBJECTS,
    apply_budget,
    bind,
    draw_ending,
    drop,
    object_reads,
    refute,
    registry_events_read,
    unverify,
)
```

`render.PAD_PVC_OBJECTS` (added to `render.py` in Step 2, right after `ENDINGS`) is a
module-level constant: 9 `Object`s, one per name in `names.PAD_PVCS`, each `kind="pvc"`,
`placement="unmounted"` so `rules.attribute` always rules them out and they can never affect
a decided cause, `fresh=o.Fresh(how="not_read")` so no rng draw ever touches them,
`intent="decoy"`. Building the 9 objects lives in `render.py`, not in `cases.py` — this
keeps the "no builder imports `objects` directly" seam intact.

```python
def test_truncated_menu_overflows_the_render_cap():
    e = _entry("worker-containerd-stop")
    n = names_mod.draw(random.Random(11))
    ex_trunc = cases.truncated(e, n, random.Random(11))
    ex_attr = cases.attributed(e, n, random.Random(11))
    # The padded menu carries the attributed menu's candidates plus 9 always-
    # ruled-out PVC decoys -- more than render_candidates' per-workload cap
    # (MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_WORKLOAD), so the padding is only proof of overflow
    # if it actually pushes the total past that cap.
    assert ex_attr.user.count("considered") + 9 > c.MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_WORKLOAD
    # render_candidates stops rendering at the cap and prints the marker
    # instead of a 9th "considered" line, so the truncated example's own
    # count is the cap itself, not attr_count + 9.
    assert ex_trunc.user.count("considered") == c.MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_WORKLOAD
    assert c.TRUNCATION_MARKER in ex_trunc.user
```

Today's `truncated` (`src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py`, lines 217-229):

```python
def truncated(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    w = _workload(e, n, _candidates(e, n, rng), confidence=_confidence(e))
    label, content = e.reads[0]
    filler = _fmt(content, n) + ("last message repeated\n" * 300)  # > 4 KiB, forces the cap
    reads = (c.EvidenceRead(label=_fmt(label, n), content=filler),)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    cause = _fmt(e.winner_cause, n)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": cause, "confidence": "low",
             "rationale": "The evidence was truncated, so the candidate is only weakly confirmed."}]
    summary = f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is probably failing from: {cause}.\nEvidence was truncated; treat with caution."
    return Example(case="truncated", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta={"case": "truncated", "entry": e.key, "expected_cause": cause,
                         "expected_confidence": "low"})
```

Replace with:

```python
def truncated(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    real_menu = _option_a_menu(n, e.objects, rng)
    padded = real_menu + PAD_PVC_OBJECTS
    budgeted = apply_budget(padded, workload_order=(0,) * len(padded),
                            entry_or_scenario_key=e.key, allow_overflow=True)
    raw = rules.attribute(budgeted, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod, issue=e.issue)
    candidates = _to_contract_candidates(raw, rules.decide(raw))
    winner = c.Candidate(cause=_fmt(e.winner_cause, n), verdict="attributed",
                          reason=_fmt(e.winner_reason, n))
    cands = list(candidates) + [winner]
    rng.shuffle(cands)
    w = _workload(e, n, tuple(cands), confidence=_confidence(e))
    reads = object_reads(budgeted, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    cause = _fmt(e.winner_cause, n)
    rationale = "The evidence was truncated, so the candidate is only weakly confirmed."
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": cause, "confidence": "low",
             "rationale": rationale}]
    summary = f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is probably failing from: {cause}.\nEvidence was truncated; treat with caution."
    key = f"{n.ns}/{n.name}"
    result = rules.Result(decided=True, cause=cause, outcome="confirmed",
                          evidence=rationale, group_key="", group_text="", decisions=())
    wm = workload_meta(result, expected_cause=cause, own_cause_keywords=[])
    decoys = [cand.cause for cand in cands if cand.cause != cause]
    meta = {"case": "truncated", "entry": e.key, "expected_cause": cause,
            "expected_confidence": "low"}
    meta.update(prompt_meta({key: wm}, label="none", decoy_by_workload={key: decoys}))
    return Example(case="truncated", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta=meta)
```

- [ ] **Step 23: `none_of_these_case` — every declared object refuted, one read each**

New shared helper, added next to `_option_a_menu`/`_decoy_result` (this step's addition —
`cases.py` has no `_refuted_menu` today):

```python
def _refuted_menu(n: Names, objects: tuple) -> tuple:
    """Bind every declared object, then refute it unconditionally. No Option-A
    draw: R38's "menu is wrong" case types keep their designed ending.
    """
    names = n._asdict()
    return tuple(refute(bind(obj, names)) for obj in objects)
```

Today's `none_of_these_case` (`src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py`, lines 185-198):

```python
def none_of_these_case(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    w = _workload(e, n, _candidates(e, n, rng), confidence=_confidence(e))
    # The finding stays, but every read now shows the contradiction.
    label = _fmt(e.reads[0][0], n)
    reads = (c.EvidenceRead(label=label, content=_fmt(e.contradiction, n)),)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": c.NONE_OF_THESE,
             "confidence": "medium",
             "rationale": "The evidence contradicts every listed candidate rather than "
                          "supporting one."}]
    summary = (f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing, but the evidence rules out the listed causes.\n"
               "A closer look at the workload is needed.")
    return Example(case="none_of_these", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta={"case": "none_of_these", "entry": e.key,
                         "expected_cause": c.NONE_OF_THESE, "expected_confidence": "medium"})
```

Replace with:

```python
def none_of_these_case(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    menu = _refuted_menu(n, e.objects)
    raw = rules.attribute(menu, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod, issue=e.issue)
    result = rules.decide(raw)  # always undecided: refuted alone never wins
    candidates = _to_contract_candidates(raw, result)
    w = _workload(e, n, candidates, confidence=_confidence(e))
    reads = object_reads(menu, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": c.NONE_OF_THESE,
             "confidence": "medium",
             "rationale": "The evidence contradicts every listed candidate rather than "
                          "supporting one."}]
    summary = (f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing, but the evidence rules out the listed causes.\n"
               "A closer look at the workload is needed.")
    key = f"{n.ns}/{n.name}"
    wm = workload_meta(result, expected_cause=c.NONE_OF_THESE, own_cause_keywords=[])
    decoys = [cand.cause for cand in candidates]
    meta = {"case": "none_of_these", "entry": e.key,
            "expected_cause": c.NONE_OF_THESE, "expected_confidence": "medium"}
    meta.update(prompt_meta({key: wm}, label="none", decoy_by_workload={key: decoys}))
    return Example(case="none_of_these", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta=meta)
```

```python
def empty_candidates(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names) -> Example:
    names = n._asdict()
    bound = tuple(bind(obj, names) for obj in e.objects)
    remaining = bound
    for obj in bound:
        remaining = drop(remaining, obj)  # ends at (); exercised for its own sake, the
                                           # menu stays empty by direct construction below
    w = _workload(e, n, (), confidence="")
    reads = (c.EvidenceRead(label=_fmt(e.reads[0][0], n), content=_fmt(e.reads[0][1], n)),)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    cause = _fmt(e.own_cause, n)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": cause, "confidence": "medium",
             "rationale": _fmt(e.rationale, n)
                          + " The candidate list shown did not include this cause."}]
    summary = f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing: {cause}.\nNo deterministic candidates were available."
    key = f"{n.ns}/{n.name}"
    result = rules.Result(decided=False, cause="", outcome="", evidence="",
                          group_key="", group_text="", decisions=())
    wm = workload_meta(result, expected_cause=cause,
                       own_cause_keywords=list(e.own_cause_keywords))
    meta = {"case": "empty_candidates", "entry": e.key, "expected_cause": cause,
            "expected_confidence": "medium",
            "expected_own_keywords": list(e.own_cause_keywords)}
    meta.update(prompt_meta({key: wm}, label="none", decoy_by_workload={key: []}))
    return Example(case="empty_candidates", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta=meta)
```

The reads panel deliberately does NOT go through `object_reads`: `empty_candidates` keeps
ONE prose read, the entry's own `e.reads[0]`, untouched — objects being empty is a statement
about the CANDIDATE MENU, not the evidence
panel, so the row's one read stays exactly what `_reads(e, n)[0]` would have produced today,
formatted by hand here since `_reads` stays alive a while longer: `multi()` (Step 36) is
the last remaining caller, and `_reads` itself is deleted at the end of that step, once
`multi` has moved off it too.

`drop(objects, obj)` removes `obj` from an objects tuple — a filter, not a per-object
transform like `refute`/`unverify`. The loop above binds every declared object, then drops
each one from the running tuple in turn, so `remaining` ends at `()`; its value still feeds
nothing else — `w`'s candidates come from the direct `()` passed to `_workload` two lines
below, and the one evidence read comes from catalog prose, not from any object.
`empty_candidates` never calls `rules.attribute`/`rules.decide` at all: unlike every other
row in this part, the "zero candidates" behavior comes from never building a candidate menu
in the first place, not from `decide()` ruling everything out.

```python
def wrong_attribution(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    """TRAINING case: the deterministic pass tagged the wrong candidate.

    The evidence is untouched and still supports the catalog winner, but the
    trace hands `attributed` to the decoy. Shuffling alone would not reach
    this: it defeats position while leaving the tag a perfectly reliable
    signal, so a shuffle-only retrain buys a tag-copier instead of a
    position-copier. This case is what makes the tag merely *usually* right,
    which is what it is in the field.
    """
    cands = list(_swapped_candidates(e, n))
    rng.shuffle(cands)
    ex = _winner_example(e, n, tuple(cands), _reads(e, n), "wrong_attribution",
                         {"decoy_cause": _decoy_cause(e, n)})
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": _fmt(e.winner_cause, n),
             "confidence": _confidence(e),
             "rationale": _fmt(e.rationale, n)
                          + " The deterministic pass attributed a different cause, but the"
                            " evidence supports this one."}]
    summary = (f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing: {_fmt(e.winner_cause, n)}.\n"
               "The deterministic pass attributed a different cause.")
    return Example(case=ex.case, group=ex.group, system=ex.system, user=ex.user,
                   assistant=_answer(rows, summary), meta=ex.meta)
```

Replace with:

```python
def wrong_attribution(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    """TRAINING case: the deterministic pass tagged the wrong candidate.

    Every declared object is scanned as `attributed` — `rules.attribute` tags a
    candidate that way from its own placement, before any read happens — but the
    copy `object_reads` renders from is `refute`d first, so the fresh evidence no
    longer backs the tag. `rules.decide` re-checks that same refuted copy and
    finds it refuted too, so the rules pass never decides this row: the
    `attributed` label on the menu is a stale scan-time trace, not a live read.
    The expected answer is the entry's own cause, which the menu never lists at
    all — passing this row means noticing the tag and the fresh read disagree,
    not trusting either one by default.
    """
    names = n._asdict()
    declared = tuple(bind(obj, names) for obj in e.objects)
    refuted = tuple(refute(obj) for obj in declared)
    raw = rules.attribute(refuted, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod, issue=e.issue)
    result = rules.decide(raw)
    candidates = _to_contract_candidates(raw, result)
    cands = list(candidates)
    rng.shuffle(cands)
    w = _workload(e, n, tuple(cands), confidence=_confidence(e))
    reads = object_reads(refuted, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    cause = _fmt(e.own_cause, n)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": cause, "confidence": _confidence(e),
             "rationale": _fmt(e.rationale, n)
                          + " The deterministic pass attributed a different cause, but the"
                            " evidence supports this one."}]
    summary = (f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing: {cause}.\n"
               "The deterministic pass attributed a different cause.")
    key = f"{n.ns}/{n.name}"
    wm = workload_meta(result, expected_cause=cause,
                       own_cause_keywords=list(e.own_cause_keywords))
    decoys = [cand.cause for cand in candidates]
    meta = {"case": "wrong_attribution", "entry": e.key, "expected_cause": cause,
            "expected_confidence": _confidence(e)}
    meta.update(prompt_meta({key: wm}, label="none", decoy_by_workload={key: decoys}))
    return Example(case="wrong_attribution", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta=meta)
```

`rules.attribute`'s verdict tag comes from an object's declared placement (`"on"`/`"mounted"`),
never from its `Fresh` state, so calling it on the `refuted` copies still yields
`verdict="attributed"` for every candidate — the tag is placement-only and `refute` never
touches placement. `rules.decide` is the one that reads `Fresh`, and a `refute`d object always
reads back refuted, so `result.decisions` carries `outcome="refuted"` for each, and
`result.decided` is `False` (a refuted candidate never wins alone, per `rules.decide`'s own
contract). No object is exempt: on `worker-containerd-stop` the node object that
Task 5 declares `intent="cause"` is refuted here exactly like the decoy pvc — graceful
degradation carves out no exception for the cause object in this builder, whatever its
declared intent: the row's whole point is that the menu's tag is not trustworthy, on ANY
object it lists.

Two things changed from today's shape that are worth calling out plainly: there is no longer a
hand-built `ruled_out`-tagged winner candidate appended to the menu (the winner was never an
`Object`; the new menu is built entirely from `e.objects`, so a row on an entry with an empty
`e.objects` tuple degrades to an empty candidate menu rather than raising, matching the
graceful-degradation rule — `wrong_attribution` was never in the raise-on-empty list, unlike
`positional_probe`/`misattribution_probe`/`contradiction_probe`), and the expected cause moves
from `e.winner_cause` to `e.own_cause` per R33's "expected: the own cause" rule for this whole
family. `_decoy_cause` and its `decoy_cause` meta key are retired for this builder along with
it — `_decoy_cause` itself is deleted from the module in Step 31 once every caller has moved
off it.

Today's `positional_probe` (`src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py`, lines 265-280):

```python
def positional_probe(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names) -> Example:
    """EVAL-ONLY: the honest `attributed` tag, but the winner placed LAST.

    Deterministic — never shuffled — because the whole point is to hold
    position fixed against the correct answer. A model reading the evidence
    or even just the tag scores this; a model answering by index cannot.
    """
    if not e.losers:
        raise ValueError(f"positional_probe needs at least one loser: {e.key}")
    losers = tuple(c.Candidate(cause=_fmt(cause, n), verdict=verdict, reason=_fmt(reason, n))
                   for cause, verdict, reason in e.losers)
    winner = c.Candidate(cause=_fmt(e.winner_cause, n), verdict="attributed",
                         reason=_fmt(e.winner_reason, n))
    return _winner_example(e, n, losers + (winner,), _reads(e, n), "positional_probe",
                           {"decoy_cause": _decoy_cause(e, n)})
```

Replace with:

```python
def positional_probe(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names, rng: random.Random) -> Example:
    """EVAL-ONLY: the honest `attributed` tag, but the winner placed LAST.

    Deterministic ordering — decoys in declaration order, winner always last —
    because the whole point is to hold position fixed against the correct
    answer. A model reading the evidence or even just the tag scores this; a
    model answering by index cannot. Endings are still drawn per decoy through
    Option A, same as `attributed`, so the decoy menu is exactly as varied —
    only the ORDER is pinned.
    """
    if not e.objects:
        raise ValueError(f"positional_probe needs at least one object: {e.key}")
    menu = _option_a_menu(n, e.objects, rng)
    candidates, _result = _decoy_result(e, n, menu)
    winner = c.Candidate(cause=_fmt(e.winner_cause, n), verdict="attributed",
                         reason=_fmt(e.winner_reason, n))
    reads = object_reads(menu, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod)
    return _winner_example(e, n, tuple(candidates) + (winner,), reads, "positional_probe")
```

The declaration order comes straight from `_option_a_menu`, which walks `e.objects` in the
order the catalog entry declares them — `bind`/`draw_ending` never reorder, so "declaration
order" and "the order `_option_a_menu` returns" are the same thing by construction; no explicit
sort is needed here. `_decoy_result` folds in the read-budget/`rules.attribute`/`rules.decide`
pass exactly as `attributed` uses it (Step 20), so the decided line comes from the same rules
path. The `decoy_cause` meta key is dropped for the same reason as Step 26: `_decoy_cause` reads
`e.losers`, which this builder no longer touches.

`rng` widens the signature from today's `(e, n)` to `(e, n, rng)` — every call site in
`generate.py` that builds a `positional_probe` example must pass an `rng.Random`, the same one
already threaded through `attributed`/`injection`/`truncated`. That call-site update happens
later in this plan, not in this step; this step only changes `cases.py` itself.

Today's `misattribution_probe` (`src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py`, lines 282-292):

```python
def misattribution_probe(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names) -> Example:
    """EVAL-ONLY: tag and position BOTH point away from the evidence.

    The adversarial slice. Deterministic ordering, decoy first, decoy tagged
    `attributed`, evidence unchanged and still supporting the winner. See
    _swapped_candidates for why this is a lower bound on tag-following.
    """
    if not e.losers:
        raise ValueError(f"misattribution_probe needs at least one loser: {e.key}")
    return _winner_example(e, n, _swapped_candidates(e, n), _reads(e, n),
                           "misattribution_probe", {"decoy_cause": _decoy_cause(e, n)})
```

Replace with:

```python
def _ruled_out_menu(n: Names, objects: tuple) -> tuple:
    """Bind every declared object, then force each one to the value
    `rules.attribute` rules out on sight: `placement="off"` for a node,
    `placement="unmounted"` for a pvc, `scan_reason="1"` for a registry (one
    puller, below `REGISTRY_THRESHOLD`). Every kind `attribute` can consider
    is forced into a shape it rules out here, so `decide()` — which skips a
    ruled-out candidate entirely and never re-checks it — can never pick any
    of them; see `test_ruled_out_menu_forces_registry_decoy_below_threshold`
    below.
    """
    names = n._asdict()
    out = []
    for obj in objects:
        bound = bind(obj, names)
        if bound.kind == "node":
            bound = dataclasses.replace(bound, placement="off")
        elif bound.kind == "pvc":
            bound = dataclasses.replace(bound, placement="unmounted")
        elif bound.kind == "registry":
            bound = dataclasses.replace(bound, scan_reason="1")
        out.append(bound)
    return tuple(out)


def misattribution_probe(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names) -> Example:
    """EVAL-ONLY: position points away from the evidence, and the menu is honest.

    Every decoy is forced to a value `rules.attribute` rules out on sight —
    `placement` for a node or pvc, `scan_reason` for a registry — so each
    candidate carries `verdict="ruled_out"` from the start —
    there is no misleading `attributed` tag to follow here, unlike the old
    shape. The adversarial part is purely positional: the decoy still leads,
    the evidence still does not support any listed candidate, and the correct
    answer — the entry's own cause — is not on the menu at all. A model that
    defaults to "trust the first candidate" or "trust the attributed one"
    fails this row either way; a model reading the ruled-out reasons and
    naming the real cause passes it.
    """
    if not e.objects:
        raise ValueError(f"misattribution_probe needs at least one object: {e.key}")
    menu = _ruled_out_menu(n, e.objects)
    candidates, result = _decoy_result(e, n, menu)
    w = _workload(e, n, candidates, confidence=_confidence(e))
    reads = object_reads(menu, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    cause = _fmt(e.own_cause, n)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": cause, "confidence": _confidence(e),
             "rationale": _fmt(e.rationale, n)}]
    summary = f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing: {cause}.\n{_fmt(e.recommendation, n).capitalize()}."
    key = f"{n.ns}/{n.name}"
    wm = workload_meta(result, expected_cause=cause,
                       own_cause_keywords=list(e.own_cause_keywords))
    decoys = [cand.cause for cand in candidates]
    meta = {"case": "misattribution_probe", "entry": e.key, "expected_cause": cause,
            "expected_confidence": _confidence(e)}
    meta.update(prompt_meta({key: wm}, label="none", decoy_by_workload={key: decoys}))
    return Example(case="misattribution_probe", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta=meta)
```

Registry decoys are forced un-decidable the same way node and pvc decoys already are:
`_ruled_out_menu` now sets `scan_reason="1"` on a registry decoy, one puller, below
`REGISTRY_THRESHOLD` (2). In `rules.attribute`, a registry candidate below
`REGISTRY_THRESHOLD` is marked `verdict="ruled_out"` before `rules.decide` ever runs, and
`decide()` skips a ruled-out candidate entirely — never re-checked, never placed in
`decisions`. So a registry decoy can no longer be picked by `rules.decide`, matching the row's
stated outcome ("never decided by the decoy (ruled out)") for all three object kinds, the same
way `refute()` already protects `multi_misattribution_probe` (Step 30). This step keeps the
cheaper `scan_reason="1"` forcing rather than reusing `refute()`, so it does not also rewrite
`fresh.literal`, which `registry_events_read` still reads for this menu.

Add to `tests/test_cases.py`:

```python
def test_ruled_out_menu_forces_registry_decoy_below_threshold():
    """A registry decoy that would otherwise be a real confirmed cause (enough pullers,
    a connection literal) is forced below REGISTRY_THRESHOLD, so attribute() rules it out
    and decide() can never pick it."""
    from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import objects as o
    from kubeagent_verdict.dataset import rules

    n = names_mod.draw(random.Random(7))
    registry = o.Object(kind="registry", name="registry.example.com", scan_reason="5",
                        placement="", fresh=o.Fresh(how="read", literal="dial tcp"))
    menu = cases._ruled_out_menu(n, (registry,))
    assert int(menu[0].scan_reason) < rules.REGISTRY_THRESHOLD
    raw = rules.attribute(menu, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod, issue="ImagePullBackOff")
    assert raw[0].verdict == "ruled_out"
    assert rules.decide(raw).decided is False
```

Today's `contradiction_probe` (`src/kubeagent_verdict/dataset/cases.py`, lines 295-352):

```python
def contradiction_probe(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names) -> Example:
    """EVAL-ONLY: tag, position and phrase length all point away from the answer.

    The three probes above perturb only the candidate menu. Each entry's
    issue/reason/evidence finding block is byte-identical across every case
    built from that entry, and no catalog entry is ever held out — all
    nineteen appear in train, val and test alike. So a model that ignores the
    menu completely and recites a memorised entry-to-winner lookup table,
    keyed on that untouched finding block, scores 1.0 cause accuracy on all
    three probes with a decoy rate of 0.0 and the narrowest possible length
    split. Every existing release decider reads clean for it.

    This row was built to be the one that cannot be answered that way: the
    reads contradict the catalog winner (as in `none_of_these`), the decoy
    leads and carries `attributed` (as in `misattribution_probe`), and the
    correct answer — "none of these" — is on no candidate line, so it can be
    neither copied nor pointed at.

    IT DOES NOT DO THAT. The claim is retracted here rather than deleted,
    because the measurement is worth more than the intention. Negative control
    v4 scored the known-broken first tune on this slice: 1.0 cause, 0.0 decoy
    — a clean pass by a model proven elsewhere to follow the `attributed` tag
    79% of the time, emitting the expected rationale and summary VERBATIM. The
    confound is that this builder reuses `none_of_these_case`'s read
    construction exactly — same label, same `e.contradiction` content — and
    `none_of_these` is 15% of the curriculum, so the contradiction sentence is
    itself a memorised trigger for a memorised answer template. Holding the
    adversarial menu roughly fixed and changing only the read text moves cause
    accuracy from 0.1579 (`misattribution_probe`) and 0.4737
    (`wrong_attribution`) to 1.0 here. The menu is what this row perturbs, and
    the menu is what such a model never reads.

    So: an index-copier, a tag-copier and a word counter do score zero here,
    and that much the slice is kept for. An entry-lookup table does not. No
    slice built from this catalog can rule one out while every entry appears
    in training — that needs held-out entries and a retrain, which v0.1.0 does
    not have. Do not read a pass here as evidence that the model reasons.
    """
    if not e.losers:
        raise ValueError(f"contradiction_probe needs at least one loser: {e.key}")
    if not e.contradiction:
        raise ValueError(f"contradiction_probe needs a contradiction read: {e.key}")
    w = _workload(e, n, _swapped_candidates(e, n), confidence=_confidence(e))
    reads = (c.EvidenceRead(label=_fmt(e.reads[0][0], n),
                            content=_fmt(e.contradiction, n)),)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": c.NONE_OF_THESE,
             "confidence": "medium",
             "rationale": "The evidence contradicts every listed candidate rather than "
                          "supporting one."}]
    summary = (f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing, but the evidence rules out the listed causes.\n"
               "A closer look at the workload is needed.")
    return Example(case="contradiction_probe", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta={"case": "contradiction_probe", "entry": e.key,
                         "expected_cause": c.NONE_OF_THESE,
                         "expected_confidence": "medium",
                         "decoy_cause": _decoy_cause(e, n)})
```

Replace with:

```python
def _contradiction_menu(n: Names, objects: tuple) -> tuple[tuple, tuple]:
    """Bind every declared object, then `unverify` it with the one ending its
    kind always gets here (R38) — never drawn, never confirmed, never cleanly
    refuted. Returns `(bound, unverified)`: `bound` keeps each object's own
    declared ending — for a registry object, its own image-error literal,
    before this probe overwrites it — and `unverified` is what the candidate
    menu and every other read actually show.
    """
    names = n._asdict()
    endings = {"node": "lease", "pvc": "read_failed", "registry": "auth"}
    bound = tuple(bind(obj, names) for obj in objects)
    unverified = tuple(unverify(obj, endings[obj.kind]) for obj in bound)
    return bound, unverified


def contradiction_probe(e: CatalogEntry, n: Names) -> Example:
    """EVAL-ONLY: the rules pass can look confident and still be wrong.

    Every declared object is `unverify`d with the one ending its kind always
    gets here — `"lease"` for a node, `"read_failed"` for a pvc, `"auth"` for
    a registry — never drawn, never confirmed, never cleanly refuted.
    `rules.decide` still runs its ordinary logic over that unverified state:
    with nothing confirmed, the first unverified candidate wins by the same
    rule that lets a genuinely uncertain read carry a workload elsewhere in
    the curriculum, so this row can come back `decided=True`, pointing at the
    decoy's cause. That is the trap: a reader who trusts the decided flag on
    sight gets the wrong answer. The correct answer is `c.NONE_OF_THESE` —
    the row's real evidence (a fresh two-line registry-events read for a
    registry decoy, or the catalog's own untouched `e.contradiction` prose
    alongside the unverified object's read for a node or pvc decoy) is what
    actually rules every listed candidate out, and it never appears as a
    candidate line itself, so it can be neither copied nor pointed at.
    """
    if not e.objects:
        raise ValueError(f"contradiction_probe needs at least one object: {e.key}")
    if not e.contradiction:
        raise ValueError(f"contradiction_probe needs a contradiction read: {e.key}")
    bound, menu = _contradiction_menu(n, e.objects)
    candidates, result = _decoy_result(e, n, menu)
    w = _workload(e, n, candidates, confidence=_confidence(e))
    own_line = _fmt(e.contradiction, n)
    used_registry = False
    reads = []
    for bound_obj, obj in zip(bound, menu):
        if obj.kind == "registry":
            reads.append(registry_events_read(obj, ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod, image=n.image,
                                               own_line=bound_obj.fresh.literal))
            used_registry = True
        else:
            reads.extend(object_reads((obj,), ns=n.ns, pod=n.pod))
    if not used_registry:
        reads.append(c.EvidenceRead(label=_fmt(e.reads[0][0], n), content=own_line))
    reads = tuple(reads)
    user = c.build_user_message(None, None, "", _service_issues(e, n), (w,), reads)
    rows = [{"workload": f"{n.ns}/{n.name}", "cause": c.NONE_OF_THESE,
             "confidence": "medium",
             "rationale": "The evidence contradicts every listed candidate rather than "
                          "supporting one."}]
    summary = (f"{n.ns}/{n.name} is failing, but the evidence rules out the listed causes.\n"
               "A closer look at the workload is needed.")
    key = f"{n.ns}/{n.name}"
    wm = workload_meta(result, expected_cause=c.NONE_OF_THESE, own_cause_keywords=[])
    decoys = [cand.cause for cand in candidates]
    meta = {"case": "contradiction_probe", "entry": e.key,
            "expected_cause": c.NONE_OF_THESE,
            "expected_confidence": "medium"}
    meta.update(prompt_meta({key: wm}, label="none", decoy_by_workload={key: decoys}))
    return Example(case="contradiction_probe", group=f"{e.key}:{n.ns}/{n.name}",
                   system=c.SYSTEM_PROMPT, user=user, assistant=_answer(rows, summary),
                   meta=meta)
```

On `worker-containerd-stop` — the only entry with an `intent="cause"` object and no registry
object of its own — the node decoy path is what fires: `used_registry` stays `False`, so the
row carries the node's own `object_reads` entry (broken/inconclusive, per `"lease"`) PLUS the
untouched `e.contradiction` prose read, matching fact 4's "the node read stays broken, the
contradiction lives in a different read": this entry needs no registry object for
`contradiction_probe` to be satisfiable. The registry branch passes `image=n.image` — the
workload's own image reference, the value every other `registry_events_read`/`object_reads`
call site in this file passes — never `obj.name`, which is only the registry's bare address
(for example `registry.example.com`) and would read as a mismatched, unrelated image. And
`own_line` comes from `bound_obj.fresh.literal` — the registry object's own declared
image-error literal, read off `bound` before `_contradiction_menu` unverifies it to `"auth"` —
never `e.contradiction`: that catalog sentence is prose written for the node/pvc fallback path,
not a "Failed to pull image" literal, and reusing it here would put a sentence, not a literal,
into the second `Failed:` line's `formatEvents` slot.
